## 2026-10-09 — hls-est: Vitis storage model (LUTRAM >= 1024, RAMB18 aspect modes / true-dual-port x18 cost, RAM_1WNR replication, 17-slot port model, invariant-load hoist)
- **Commit:** 342b39dfc (code, tests, notes and this entry; branch `hls-est-vitis-match`); this hash line: the following changelog-only commit
- **Files:** include/circt/Dialect/Resource/Interfaces/MemoryDS.h, lib/Dialect/Resource/Interfaces/MemoryInterface.cpp, include/circt/Dialect/Resource/Passes/MemrefBankClassification.h, lib/Dialect/Resource/Passes/MemrefBankClassification.cpp, lib/Dialect/Resource/Passes/AffineToLoopScheduleModified.cpp, include/circt/Conversion/Passes.td, lib/Dialect/Resource/Passes/BRAMAnalysis.cpp; new lit tests test/hls-est/Passes/{replication_1wnr,replication_two_stores,invariant_load_hoist,lutram_threshold,aspect_modes}.mlir; updated expectations test/hls-est/Passes/{fast_schedule,bram_debug_json,bram_debug_text,bram_debug_status}.mlir; tools/hls-est-validate/VITIS_NOTES.md (hls-est status of N4/N5/N6), OPEN_ISSUES.md (O2-O5), CHANGELOG.md
- **What:**
  1. *LUTRAM threshold* (`MemoryInterface.cpp` `meetsBRAMPromotionThreshold`, `MemoryDS.h` `kBRAMPromotionBits = 1024`): `Words * exact Bits >= 1024` -> BRAM (was `> 1024`). The exclusion text reads `size N bits < 1024-bit BRAM promotion threshold (Words*Bits >= 1024 -> BRAM); distributed RAM, 0 BRAM`.
  2. *BRAM_18K per bank* (`BRAMAnalysis.cpp` `estimateBlocks(numElems, bits, wide)`): `min over m in {1,2,4,9,18,36} of ceil(W/m) * max(1, P/depth(m))`, `P = 2^ceil(log2 Words)`, `depth(m) = 16384/8192/4096/2048/1024/512`; the x36 mode only when at most one port is live per cycle (`isWideKind`: RAM_1P, RAM_S2P), x18 is the ceiling for RAM_2P/RAM_T2P and for every RAM_1WNR copy. `kBitsPerBRAM` is 18432 (parity included). The task text allowed the old pow2-width/16384-bit tiling "unless the aspect experiment note exists"; it does (`~/hls-est-work/exp-notes/aspect.md` R1/R2, `aspect_chk.md` depth rounding), so it was followed.
  3. *Replication* (`BRAMAnalysis.cpp` `analyzeReplication`): per pipelined loop that touches the bank, `need = ceil((R + W) / II)` with R/W the loads/stores per iteration (after unrolling and hoisting); `copies = max over loops of min(16, max(1, need - 1))`; a bank that some loop body (pipelined or sequential loop, not straight-line code) stores to more than once is never replicated. `copies > 1` -> kind `RAM_1WNR`, BRAM = copies x narrow cost of one copy; otherwise the per-cycle slot analysis chooses RAM_1P/2P/T2P as before, except that a replicable bank whose scheduler-packed demand exceeds 2 while `need <= 2` is priced as a 2-port RAM (Vitis spreads the accesses over the II).
  4. *Scheduler port model* (`MemrefBankClassification.cpp`, `AffineToLoopScheduleModified.cpp` `linkMemoryResource`): loads/stores are counted per innermost loop body; `replicable` iff the max stores per loop body <= 1 -> `StorageMode::OneWriteNRead`, one resource pool with `kReplicatedPortSlots` = 17 slots per cycle; otherwise `TDP` = 2; untracked memrefs (function arguments, get_global results) 2. `storageModeSlots()` is the single source of the limits, so the simplex and the greedy (`FastModuloScheduler`) paths agree; the OnePort/SDP modes are no longer produced.
  5. *Invariant loads* (`AffineToLoopSchedule::hoistInvariantLoads`, option `hoist-invariant`, default on): after `coalescePerfectNests()` and before any analysis, for each innermost loop the ops directly in its body are moved before the loop, in program order, when every operand is defined outside the loop or by an op hoisted before it: memory-effect-free speculatable ops, and `affine.load` ops whose memref no op inside the loop writes (any write/alloc/free effect or an opaque use counts as a write). Nothing under an `affine.if` moves.
  6. *Sequential scopes* keep the one-port rule of `scopeDemand`.
  7. *Debug output*: JSON `version` 2; per memref a `replication` object (`replicable`, `max_stores_per_loop`, `copies`, `limiting_loop`, `loops[{scope_loc, ii, reads, writes, need, copies}]`); `bank_math` gains `wide`, `addr_depth`, `blocks_per_copy`, and `config_width` / `width_tiles` / `depth_tiles` now mean the chosen mode m, the columns ceil(W/m) and the blocks per column; `ram_type` `RAM_1WNR` with a `ram_reason` naming the limiting loop; the text report prints a `replication:` block and the mode-based bank math.
- **Why:** VITIS_NOTES.md N4 (`replication.md`, 56 kernels: R1-R4), N5 (`lutram_chk.md`, 23 kernels), N6 (`aspect.md`, 147 kernels + `aspect_chk.md`: 2500x18 one-port = 4 only with the power-of-two depth). Corpus check of the per-bank arithmetic against every plain (non-1WNR, single-bank, >= 1024-bit) memory row of `results/vitis.json` (13391 rows): the new formula matches 13294, the old pow2/16384 tiling 12765, `ceil(bits/18432)` 13014, the same mode formula without the power-of-two depth 13090; the deep rows (73200x32 -> 256, 40200x32 -> 128, 192000x11 -> 176) are exact only with it. Why the hoist is inside the scheduler pass and not a pipeline-level `--affine-loop-invariant-code-motion`: run before `--convert-affine-to-loopschedule`, MLIR's LICM moves the 8 `buff_A[i][r]` loads out of lp2 *before* the flatten decision, the lp1/lp2 nest becomes imperfect, is not coalesced, and buff_A ends as a plain 8-BRAM RAM (total 16) where Vitis flattens lp1_lp2 and replicates (7 copies, total 64); measured on rep_inv_R08 with both orders.
- **Effect:**
  - *Standalone kernels* (`replication.md` table). Inputs: cgeist output of `~/hls-est-work/exp-src/replication/*.cpp` (scalar shim, `~/hls-est-work/t2/mlir/`), with the reduction loop `lp3` put back inside `lp2` (`~/hls-est-work/t2/mlir_vitis/`): cgeist at every `-O` level hoists the j-invariant `lp3` loop out of `lp2`, which Vitis does not do before its flatten decision (its log shows `lp1_lp2` flattened with the loads inside); the loop is tagged `hls.debug_unroll` and unrolled with `--debug-unroll` because `--auto-pipeline-unroll` (inverted rule: trip > 32 is unrolled) does not unroll the 8-trip `lp3` but does unroll the 64-trip copy-in loops, which gives 64 stores per iteration and no replication (`threshold=1` does the same). Pipeline `--affine-loop-normalize --debug-unroll --memory-banking-bram --convert-affine-to-loopschedule --bram-analysis --symbol-privatize=exclude=<top> --symbol-dce`. buff_A hls-est / Vitis: rep_inv_R02 8/8 (plain T2P), R03 16/16 (2 copies), R04 24/24 (3), R08 56/56 (7), R16 120/120 (15), R17 128/128 (16), R18 64/64 (8 copies, II 2), R32 120/120 (15, II 2), rep_wr2_R08 8/8 (T2P, lp1_lp2 II 4), rep_2n_write_R08 128/128 (8 copies with the store), rep_size_N16_R08 14/14, rep_w16_R08 28/28, rep_small_N4_R04 0/0 (3 LUTRAM copies); totals 16/24/32/64/128/136/72/128/16/136/15/32/0, all equal to Vitis. With `--infer-vitis-split-array` added: 11/13 (rep_wr2_R08 buff_A 24 = 3 port-split banks, rep_2n_write_R08 16 = 8 banks: the pass's port-pressure partitioning, the td=auto behaviour of N2, pre-empts the W > 1 rule).
  - *atax* (`~/hls-est-work/mlir/polybench/atax.bb6019e644a0a973.mlir`): canonical pipeline 67 (unchanged: `--infer-vitis-split-array` still splits buff_A into 64 banks); without it 13 = Vitis, per array buff_A 8 = Vitis (RAM_T2P: the 64-store copy-in body makes it non-replicable; schedule copy-in II 32, lp1 II 64, lp3_lp4 II 64 - *corrected by the "T2 storage review fixes" entry (D4): this line originally claimed "lp1 II 32 = Vitis", which holds only with `forward-stores=true`, where tmp1's 64 loads + 64 stores collapse to one store*), buff_y_out 2 = Vitis, buff_x 1 (Vitis 2: it issues the 64 hoisted reads on both parent ports), tmp1 2 (Vitis 1: `tmp1[i] +=` is register-promoted there); `hoist-invariant=false` gives 14.
  - *Harness subset* (`run_hlsest.py --datasets polybench,polybench_pragmas,forgebench,machsuite,spector_hls,vitis_examples -j 8 --timeout 120`, `compare.py`; before = 9deaaafde binary, same machine, `~/hls-est-work/t2/harness/{before,after,after_nosplit,after_nohoist}.*`). Canonical pipeline: exact 31/73 -> 31/73, |err| 8144 -> 8825, status counts identical (ok 75, crash 11, timeout 1); the 10 changed designs are all forgebench (activation_module/op1/op2/op3 392 -> 512, diff_dims_module_small 2368 -> 2560, diff_dims_p1 272 -> 320, diff_dims_p3 304 -> 384, mult_op_module_dot 13 -> 12, testing_impl 4 -> 6, tiled_attn_module 2336 -> 2344): their arrays are globals (accesses unattributed, so no replication; the change is item 2), and the harness's forgebench inputs are the float-shim variants whose widths differ from the ap_fixed ground truth. On the ap_fixed activation_op1 variant the live 50176x16 RAMs are now 64 = Vitis 64 each (old formula 49); the remaining error there is N1a (`FM_buffer_4` dead in Vitis, `FM_buffer_3` narrowed to a 12-bit ROM = 48). Without `--infer-vitis-split-array`: exact 34/74, |err| 8371: polybench atax 67 -> 13 =, bicg 68 -> 14 =, mvt 14 =, gesummv 22 (Vitis 135), gemm 32 (256), k2mm 56 (504), k3mm 56 (728), syr2k 56 (392), syrk 32 (256) - the gemm family needs the Vitis unroll/flatten decision (T3) before replication can reproduce its buff_A/buff_B copies; spector_hls/normals 0 -> 4 = (6/6 exact); forgebench/diff_orders_module finishes (192, Vitis 1538) instead of timing out; no design that matched before stops matching. `hoist-invariant=false`: 31/73, |err| 8827 (atax 68, bicg 69, mvt 16, gesummv 135). Regression gate (polybench_pragmas, fresh Vitis): bicg 8 =, gesummv 16 =, mvt 8 =, syrk 32 =, k2mm 56 (Vitis 504), k3mm 56 (728), syr2k 56 (392) - identical before and after under all three pipelines.
  - *Lit* `test/hls-est`: 56 tests, 27 passed (22 + the 5 new), 1 expectedly failed (memory_recurrence_xfail), 24 unresolved (pre-existing, no RUN line), 4 failed (the pre-existing forgebench CHECK-BRAM tests activation_op1 / tiled_attn_p1 / p2 / module). `fast_schedule.mlir` now expects 6 (tab: 3 reads per iteration on a replicable bank -> II 1 and 2 copies x 2 = 4; acc 2) on both schedulers.
  - *Debug invariance*: stdout identical with and without `--bram-debug --bram-debug-json` on rep_inv_R08, rep_wr2_R08, rep_2n_write_R08; `bram_debug_json.mlir` keeps its `diff` RUN line.
- **Not adopted (needs Peter's decision):** dropping `--infer-vitis-split-array` from the default pipeline (+3 exact, nothing lost; its port-pressure partitioning is the td=auto behaviour while the ground truth is td=off, see N2/N7 and OPEN_ISSUES.md O2). Also filed: O3 (sequential scopes use both ports in Vitis), O4 (globals are neither classified nor replicated), O5 (cgeist hoists invariant inner loops).

## 2026-10-09 — hls-est: T1 perf review fixes (O(log II) recurrence search, dependence-path correction, file mode)
- **Commit:** 97cf18a10 (branch `hls-est-vitis-match`); this hash line: the following changelog-only commit
- **Files:** lib/Dialect/Resource/Passes/AffineToLoopScheduleModified.cpp, lib/Analysis/DependenceAnalysis.cpp (mode only), test/hls-est/Passes/fast_schedule_recurrence.mlir (new), test/hls-est/Passes/memory_recurrence_xfail.mlir (new, XFAIL), tools/hls-est-validate/OPEN_ISSUES.md (new), tools/hls-est-validate/CHANGELOG.md
- **What:** the three findings of the review of 99ac05534 / d2a4d3a5c.
  - D2 (`FastModuloScheduler`): the recurrence-feasible II is found by `recMinII()` — galloping from ResMinII (lo+1, lo+2, lo+4, …) then bisection — over the positive-cycle longest-path test (`asapTimes()`), which is monotone in II because a cycle's slack is distance_sum·II − latency_sum and every cycle has distance ≥ 1 (`build()` rejects distance-0 cycles); the sum of all op latencies bounds the search. Before, every candidate II from ResMinII upwards re-ran the O(n·E) relaxation, cost ∝ (RecMinII − ResMinII)·n·E. The +1 bump loop now starts at the recurrence-feasible II and only resolves placement conflicts, so `maxIIBumps` (4096) counts from there instead of from ResMinII (a recurrence more than 4096 above ResMinII used to fall back to the simplex; no measured input is near that).
  - D1 (record correction, no code change): the `replaceOp()` re-keying of 99ac05534 is inert — `runOnOperation` copies `MemoryDependenceAnalysis` and lowers the affine accesses in the copy, while `CyclicSchedulingAnalysis` reads the AnalysisManager's cached instance keyed by the erased affine ops — so no loop-carried memory dependence reaches either scheduler, before or after 99ac05534 (`a[i] = a[i-1] * k` schedules at II 1 on both). The T1 report's "memory dependences now reliably reach the scheduler" was wrong; the 99ac05534 entry below carries the correction, OPEN_ISSUES.md O1 files the activation (the reviewer's one-line `auto &` experiment delivered the recurrences but failed 16 of the 42 Estimation inputs with "problem is infeasible" / "multiple sinks detected"), and `memory_recurrence_xfail.mlir` encodes the expected II 6 as an XFAIL test that turns into an XPASS when it is fixed.
  - D3: `lib/Analysis/DependenceAnalysis.cpp` back to mode 100644 (99ac05534 had made it 100755). Also fills in the commit hashes of the 99ac05534 entry.
- **Why:** review of 99ac05534 (D2 reproducer: the reviewer's `gen_patho.py`, an iter_args mulf chain of RecMinII 200–800 next to 200–800 loads). No Vitis evidence involved; no modelling change.
- **Effect:** no estimate changes. D2 reproducer (base pipeline; greedy path because > 150 limited ops), before → after on the same machine (load ≈ 3): 450 ops / RecMinII 200: 0.76 → 0.14 s; 900 / 400: 5.7 → 0.62 s; 1700 / 400: 22.5 → 1.9 s; 1800 / 800: 45.0 → 1.9 s; II unchanged (200 / 400 / 400 / 800). `fast_schedule_recurrence.mlir`: iter_args recurrences of latency 4 / 9 / 12 / 26 over ResMinII 1 give II 4 / 9 / 12 / 26 on both the greedy and the simplex scheduler. Base pipeline on the 42 `test/hls-est/Estimation` inputs vs. the reviewer's HEAD snapshots (`~/hls-est-work/t1/snap-rev-default`, `snap-rev-fast0`): 42/42 identical (rc and total) for the default and for `fast-threshold=0`, and default = fast0 42/42 (35 ok, 5 exit 1, 2 abort, all pre-existing; wall 18 s / 1.3 s). Canonical pipeline on `~/hls-est-work/mlir/polybench*` (32 files): every total identical to the reviewer's `timing.txt` (atax 67, bicg 68, gemm 328, gesummv 134, k2mm 464, k3mm 408, mvt 14, syr2k 576, syrk 320; pragmas bicg 8, gesummv 16, k2mm 56, k3mm 56, mvt 8, syr2k 56, syrk 32), max wall 18.8 s (k3mm). Lit `test/hls-est`: 51 tests, 22 passed (21 + fast_schedule_recurrence), 1 expectedly failed (memory_recurrence_xfail), 24 unresolved (no RUN line, pre-existing), 4 failed (the pre-existing forgebench CHECK-BRAM tests tiled_attn_p1 / p2 / module and activation_op1).
- **Reverted?:** no

## 2026-10-09 — hls-est: fold constant bank indices in banking; fast resource-bound scheduling for large bodies
- **Commit:** 99ac05534 (code, tests and this entry); d2a4d3a5c (precision of this entry); corrected by the `T1 perf review fixes` entry above
- **Files:** lib/Dialect/Resource/Passes/MemoryBankModified.cpp, lib/Dialect/Resource/Passes/AffineToLoopScheduleModified.cpp, include/circt/Conversion/Passes.td, lib/Analysis/DependenceAnalysis.cpp, include/circt/Analysis/DependenceAnalysis.h, lib/Dialect/Resource/Passes/CyclicSchedulingAnalysisModified.{cpp,h}, test/hls-est/Passes/banking_constant_operand.mlir, test/hls-est/Passes/fast_schedule.mlir; deleted the stale, checked-in `include/circt/Conversion/Passes.h.inc` (a Sep-18 generated copy that shadowed `build/clion/include/...` because `-I include` precedes the build include dir; with it present no new pass option could be compiled).
- **What:** performance only; no estimate is meant to change.
  1. *Banking* (`--memory-banking-bram`): `BankAffineLoadPattern`/`BankAffineStorePattern` now call `canonicalizeMapAndOperands` after composing the access map, so an SSA-constant index (what `--auto-pipeline-unroll` leaves for the first unrolled iteration, `%buf[%i, %c0]`) folds to a constant bank instead of being treated as rotating (f loads + f-1 `arith.select`, or f `affine.store_enable`). The same decision is also made through a stepped loop counter (`%buf[%j + 1]` with step 4 pins bank 1), which is how `--infer-vitis-split-array` already classifies it, so both passes agree on which accesses rotate. On atax the banked IR went from 252 selects + 192 store_enables to none.
  2. *Scheduling* (`--convert-affine-to-loopschedule`): new option `fast-threshold=N` (default 150). A loop whose modulo problem has more than N resource-limited ops is scheduled by a greedy resource-bound modulo list scheduler: II starts at ResMinII = max over resources of ceil(uses/limit), resource-free ASAP times are longest paths in the constraint graph (edge weight latency − distance·II; a positive cycle bumps II, which is what the simplex does when it raises its parameter T), ops are placed in a topological order of the distance-0 edges (ties by program order) at the first modulo-reservation-table slot from their ASAP time, every edge including back edges is re-verified and a violation bumps II. `fast-threshold=0` uses it for every loop; on failure it falls back to the simplex. Small bodies keep `ModuloSimplexScheduler`. New option `cse` (default on) merges duplicated side-effect-free ops (per-access index arithmetic) before the problem is built, never memory ops; this replaces an `OpPassManager` with CSE that was built but never run.
  3. *MemoryDependenceAnalysis* (lib/Analysis): accesses are grouped by memref before pairs are enumerated (the all-pairs loop over ~9000 accesses built two `MemRefAccess` objects and walked the loop nests for ~10^8 cross-memref pairs); pairs whose index along some dimension is a different constant skip the test; the Fourier–Motzkin test is memoized on everything it depends on (composed maps/operands, enclosing blocks, depth, program order), so the 128 identical `tmp1[i][j]` accesses per bank of an unrolled `+=` reduction cost one test instead of 16k; only actual dependences are recorded (every consumer filters on `hasDependence()`); `replaceOp()` re-keys the destination properly (the old code assigned the new key through the map iterator, which leaves the entry in the old key's hash bucket where a lookup by the new op cannot find it) and retargets sources through an inverted index instead of rescanning every list. [Correction, review of this commit, finding D1: this re-keying is inert in the pass. `runOnOperation` copies the analysis and lowers the affine accesses in the copy, while `CyclicSchedulingAnalysis` reads the AnalysisManager's cached instance keyed by the erased affine ops, so no loop-carried memory dependence reaches either scheduler, before or after this commit; the T1 report's statement that "memory dependences now reliably reach the scheduler" was wrong. Filed as OPEN_ISSUES.md O1.] `CyclicSchedulingAnalysis::analyzeForOp` takes the analysis by reference instead of copying it per loop. `--debug-only=memory-dependence-analysis` prints pair/memo/test counts and the time.
- **Why:** CLAUDE.md working loop needs the canonical pipeline to finish; atax took 9–16 min and every 64×64 kernel timed out. Verified with `--mlir-timing` on the split pipeline: banking itself was 0.007 s but its rotating expansion made the scheduler's input 4–64× larger; on gemm the dependence analysis then ran 1.04M FM tests (144 s) and `replaceOp` rescanned 1M records per lowered op (~60 s). No Vitis behaviour is modelled differently (VITIS_NOTES.md untouched).
- **Effect:**
  - Base pipeline (`--affine-loop-normalize --memory-banking-bram --convert-affine-to-loopschedule --bram-analysis`) on all 42 `test/hls-est/Estimation` inputs, before vs. after: 41 identical (rc and Total BRAM); the 42nd, basic/arraypartition_complete, timed out at 300 s before and now gives 0 BRAM in 21 s. Wall sum 333 s → 23 s. `fast-threshold=0` vs. default: 42/42 identical totals (wall 23 s → 2.7 s). `cse=false` vs. default: identical totals on the 41 inputs where both finish; basic/arraypartition_complete (exact scheduler, below the threshold) times out at 300 s without the pre-scheduling CSE and takes 21 s with it, so the simplex depends on it. `--bram-debug` invariance script: 35 PASS / 7 ERR (same failures in both modes, as before plus the un-timed-out case).
  - Canonical pipeline on fresh cgeist output (`~/hls-est-work/mlir`, 8 parallel jobs on a loaded machine), old → new binary: polybench atax >900 s → 0.2 s (67), bicg >900 s → 0.2 s (68), gemm >900 s → 14 s (328), gesummv abort after 460 s (SimplexSchedulers.cpp:1174 assertion) → 0.3 s (134), k2mm >900 s → 24 s (464), k3mm >900 s → 28 s (408), mvt abort → 0.9 s (14), syr2k >900 s → 26 s (576), syrk >900 s → 11 s (320); polybench_pragmas (all 7 kernels >300 s before): bicg 2.3 s (8), gesummv 0.9 s (16), k2mm 2.3 s (56), k3mm 3.0 s (56), mvt 2.9 s (8), syr2k 1.2 s (56), syrk 0.8 s (32). Every polybench/polybench_pragmas case is now < 60 s.
  - Greedy vs. exact on the large bodies (`fast-threshold=1000000` forces the simplex): atax 67 = 67, bicg 68 = 68 (23 s vs 0.2 s); gesummv and mvt: the simplex aborts (pre-existing assertion in its II-bump path) where the greedy gives 134 and 14; gemm/k2mm/k3mm/syr2k: the simplex exceeds 600 s; polybench_pragmas bicg 8 = 8 (the simplex needs 450-490 s), gesummv/k2mm exceed 600 s. So no case where both finish differs. Known heuristic difference, documented in fast_schedule.mlir: the simplex fixes ops in least-slack order and can raise II above ResMinII when a successor was fixed before the last of its port-contending predecessors (II 4 instead of 3 on a 3-reads-per-iteration loop); the greedy scheduler keeps ResMinII.
  - Harness subset (`run_hlsest.py --datasets polybench,polybench_pragmas,forgebench,machsuite,spector_hls,vitis_examples -j 8 --timeout 120`, canonical pipeline): status ok 63 → 75, `hls-est timeout 120s` 15 → 1 (forgebench/diff_orders_module: 1922 loads before banking become 50817 loads + 48895 selects + 49408 store_enables after it, 258 identical accesses per bank over ~900 banks = 25.6M same-memref pairs; it now finishes in ~390 s / 6.6 GB with 1344 BRAM - the per-pair dependence records are the remaining hot spot), crash 9 → 11 (forgebench/conv_block_module and conv_block_op1 now reach the pre-existing `Type::getIntOrFloatBitWidth` assertion of the conv_* family instead of timing out first). polybench: 5 timeouts → 0. `compare.py`: 73 compared, 31 exact, |err| 8144 against the partial results/vitis.json.
  - Lit `test/hls-est`: 49 tests, 21 passed (19 + the two new ones), 24 unresolved (no RUN line, pre-existing), 4 failed (the pre-existing forgebench CHECK-BRAM tests). Output is run-to-run deterministic (5 inputs × 3 runs identical, before and after).
- **Reverted?:** no

## 2026-10-09 — hls-est: debug mode review fixes
- **Commit:** 93dba77f4 (branch `hls-est-vitis-match`); this entry: the following changelog-only commit
- **Files:** lib/Dialect/Resource/Passes/BRAMAnalysis.cpp, lib/Dialect/Resource/Passes/MemoryBankModified.cpp, lib/Dialect/Resource/Interfaces/MemoryInterface.cpp, include/circt/Dialect/Resource/Interfaces/MemoryDS.h, test/hls-est/Passes/bram_debug_provenance.mlir (new), test/hls-est/Passes/bram_debug_status.mlir (new), test/hls-est/Passes/bram_debug_text.mlir, tools/hls-est-validate/CHANGELOG.md
- **What:** the six findings of the review of fa522b385. (1) A memref with a dynamic extent gets `status = "dynamic_shape"` (the status existed but was unreachable; such memrefs were reported as `zero_size`). (2) An unwritable `--bram-debug-json` path is a pass failure: the stdout report is still printed in full, then `error: bram-debug-json: cannot write '<path>': <reason>` and a non-zero exit (was a warning with exit 0, detectable only by the file's absence). (3) `hls.partition` on banks records the effective `kind`, `factor` and 1-based `dim` that banking applied (a `complete` entry without a factor shows the dimension extent; a Vitis `dim = 0` pragma shows `dim = 1`); before, `dim` was the pragma text next to an effective `factor`. (4) A partitioned array without `polygeist.varname` gets `hls.bank_of = "<unnamed@file:line:col>"`, or `"<unnamed#k>"` without a location (k = its position among the function's storage declarations), so its banks aggregate in the JSON, the text report and compare.py (before: no `hls.bank_of`, each bank keyed by its id). (5) The previous entry now records its commit hash. (6) The threshold quoted in `excluded_small` reasons is `kBRAMPromotionBits`, moved to `MemoryDS.h` and used by `contributesToBRAMCost`, so the explanation cannot go stale; the wording is `size N bits <= 1024-bit BRAM promotion threshold; kept in LUTRAM/FFs`. Not changed (informational findings of the review): get_global / function-argument banking paths remain unreachable from `runOnOperation`, there are still no entries for top-level arguments or for arrays removed by earlier passes, and `bank_math.replication` is still 1.
- **Why:** code review of fa522b385; no new Vitis evidence involved.
- **Effect:** no estimate changes. `check_debug_invariance.sh` (60 s timeout), lit pipeline (--affine-loop-normalize --memory-banking-bram --convert-affine-to-loopschedule --bram-analysis): 42 cases, 35 PASS, 0 FAIL, 7 ERR (tool failed identically with and without the flags: exit 1 chstone_xilinx/sha, flowgnn_xilinx/gin, flowgnn_xilinx/pna, machsuite/spmv_crs, spectorhls/matrix_multiplication; abort machsuite/stencil2D, spectorhls/sobel_filter_y; all pre-existing). Default pipeline: (--affine-loop-normalize --auto-pipeline-unroll --infer-vitis-split-array --memory-banking-bram --convert-affine-to-loopschedule --bram-analysis, run concurrently with another agent's sweep on the same machine): 42 cases, 31 PASS, 0 FAIL, 11 ERR = 5 x hls-est timed out at 60 s in both runs (forgebench/attention_op_p1, forgebench/tiled_attn_module, forgebench/tiled_attn_p1, forgebench/tiled_attn_p2, machsuite/fft_transpose), 4 x exit 1 (chstone_xilinx/sha, flowgnn_xilinx/gin, flowgnn_xilinx/pna, spectorhls/matrix_multiplication), 2 x abort (machsuite/stencil2D, spectorhls/sobel_filter_y); every case that produced a report produced the same stdout with and without the flags, and its JSON total equalled the report total. Lit `test/hls-est`: 49 tests, 21 passed (the two new and the two existing bram_debug tests included), 4 failed, 24 unresolved; the 28 non-passes are the pre-existing Estimation-file defects (no RUN line / undefined CHECK-BRAM).
- **Reverted?:** no

## 2026-10-09 — hls-est: --bram-debug / --bram-debug-json explanation mode; bank provenance attributes
- **Commit:** fa522b385
- **Files:** lib/Dialect/Resource/Passes/BRAMAnalysis.cpp, lib/Dialect/Resource/Passes/MemoryBankModified.cpp, lib/Dialect/Resource/Passes/InferVitisSplitArray.cpp, test/hls-est/Passes/bram_debug_json.mlir, test/hls-est/Passes/bram_debug_text.mlir, tools/hls-est-validate/check_debug_invariance.sh
- **What:** two global `llvm::cl` options (so they attach to any pipeline string): `--bram-debug` prints, to stderr after the normal report, one block per memref (id, name/varname, kind, loc, shape, element bits, words; status and exclusion reason; partition kind/factor/dim/source/bank index/count and the inference reason; every access with its scope, II, stage and store_enable flag; per-scope and combined port demand; the RAM type and why; the bank math: bits/BRAM, max width, config width, width tiles, depth/block, depth tiles, replication, blocks) and a summary sorted by BRAM; `--bram-debug-json=<path>` writes the same facts as JSON (`version`, `total_bram`, `functions`, `memrefs[]`, `summary[]`), which `compare.py` consumes. Memory banking now stamps every bank with `polygeist.varname`, `hls.preserve`, `hls.bank_of`, `hls.bank_index`, `hls.bank_count`, `hls.partition = {kind, factor, dim, source}` (the original pragma entry's fields; `source = "pragma"` when the entry has none) and `hls.partition_reason`; `infer-vitis-split-array` records `hls.partition_reason = "pipelined loop [lb..ub step s) @ loc: R reads/W writes per iteration, target II n, factor f (kind, dim d) (port-limited II a -> b)"`. No pass reads any of these attributes. Globals' accesses are not attributed (memref.global has no SSA result); the JSON says so in `ram_reason` instead of inventing data.
- **Why:** CLAUDE.md Step 3; the per-array JSON is the main debugging signal for Vitis mismatches (totals hide offsetting errors). Before this, banks created by `memory-banking-bram` had no attributes at all, so nothing could name them after the original alloca was erased.
- **Effect:** no estimate changes. `check_debug_invariance.sh` (every test/hls-est/Estimation/**/*.mlir, base pipeline, with vs. without the flags, stdout diffed): default pipeline (--affine-loop-normalize --auto-pipeline-unroll --infer-vitis-split-array --memory-banking-bram --convert-affine-to-loopschedule --bram-analysis, 60 s timeout): 42 cases, 17 PASS, 0 FAIL, 25 ERR (hls-est timed out or failed identically in both runs: 19 timeouts, 4 x exit 1, 2 x abort); lit pipeline (--affine-loop-normalize --memory-banking-bram --convert-affine-to-loopschedule --bram-analysis): 42 cases, 34 PASS, 0 FAIL, 8 ERR (1 timeout, 5 x exit 1, 2 x abort). No case produced a different stdout or a JSON total different from the report total. Lit `test/hls-est`: 45 tests, 17 passed (including the two new ones), 4 failed, 24 unresolved; the 28 non-passes are pre-existing: 24 Estimation inputs have no RUN line (unresolved) and 4 (forgebench/activation_op1, tiled_attn_p1, tiled_attn_p2, tiled_attn_module) pipe into FileCheck -check-prefix=CHECK-BRAM without defining any CHECK-BRAM: line.
- **Reverted?:** no

- **Effect:** defines the ground truth; no estimator change.
- **Reverted?:** no

## 2026-10-09 — hls-est: register DLTI and LLVM dialects; harness skeleton
- **Commit:** 3ad60fb79
- **Files:** tools/hls-est/main.cpp, tools/hls-est/CMakeLists.txt, tools/hls-est-validate/{common,designs,run_vitis,run_hlsest,vitis_parse,compare,make_sheet}.py
- **What:** hls-est now registers `mlir::DLTIDialect` and `mlir::LLVM::LLVMDialect`; the harness additionally strips the `module attributes {dlti.dl_spec = ..., llvm.data_layout = ...}` dictionary that cgeist (LLVM 18) emits, because the LLVM-23 MLIR parser in hls-est rejects the old `dlti` entry syntax (`expected a dense i64 elements attribute in the data layout entry`).
- **Why:** without this every fresh cgeist output failed to parse; the pre-generated `test/hls-est/Estimation/*.mlir` files had been hand-stripped.
- **Effect:** no estimate change (parser only). Enables the end-to-end harness.
- **Reverted?:** no

## 2026-10-11 — run_vitis: concurrent runners cannot corrupt the ground truth (coordinator)
- **Commit:** baa8eab9a, review fixes 7f670e207 (branch `hls-est-vitis-match`)
- **Files:** tools/hls-est-validate/run_vitis.py, tools/hls-est-validate/results/.gitignore
- **What:** a non-blocking flock per design work dir, inherited by the Vitis child, so a second runner skips a
  design another runner holds (status `locked`, not written) and a killed runner leaves it locked until Vitis exits;
  summary JSON updates are read-merge-write of one entry under `<out>.lock` (cached results re-read under the lock;
  `--reparse` holds it too); `--dry-run` no longer writes the summary; the timeout killer matches both separator styles
  and only its own design (it also matched sibling designs whose names extend this one).
- **Why:** two runners on one work root once corrupted 37 results; the v2 ground-truth run (665 designs, hours)
  shares the cache with agents that call run_vitis.py. A v2 `--dry-run` at 01:08 wrote 204 `dry-run` entries
  into results/vitis.json, now removed. The 143 real entries equal W8a's 22:54 snapshot. 6 genuine v2 results
  (class_ece8893_2023_spring) entered vitis.json in the repair and are kept.
- **Effect:** none on estimates or Vitis results; the independent review (no defects in the refactor; one merge defect
  and one lock gap, fixed in 7f670e207) ran on baa8eab9a.
- **Reverted?:** no
